# PROGRESS · Slide Builder 4 rebuild

Tracks [`docs/next/PLAN.md`](docs/next/PLAN.md) Part C. One line per step; a step is ticked only after the
double-check protocol of [`docs/next/PROMPTS.md`](docs/next/PROMPTS.md) (tests, clean clone, review,
numbers measured twice). Measured numbers go into the step's line.

## Steps

### M0 · Foundations on v3 (v3.4)
- [x] S0.1 · CI, PROGRESS.md, one check command — [PR #1](https://github.com/Gualbertol123/slidemaster/pull/1). `npm run check`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (unit, helper 3.8 + 3.12, e2e, and parity from S0.5). Baseline 114 Vitest / 131 helper / 15 e2e. Surprises: Chrome's sandbox needs `kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns=0` on ubuntu-24.04; runners pinned to ubuntu-24.04 (26.04 has no Python 3.8); the helper listened with a backlog of 5, so a burst of requests waited 1 s for a TCP retry (now 128, with a test); an intermittent CI e2e failure (about 1 run in 4): the tests polled the helper's files with `expect.poll`, which fails at once - not after its timeout - when the callback throws; reading a document or field that the page had not saved yet (saves leave 300 ms after a change) threw, so a CI runner that was a little slower failed on the first read. Fixed with `pollFile` (a read that throws counts as "not yet" and is retried) on every such poll, plus the exact document name (not a save's `.tmp` file); a failing e2e test now prints the page's requests, the save status and the workbook files. Found with 6 parallel CI copies and a helper request log (temporary, removed). verified: tests ✓ clean clone ✓ review ✓

### M1 · The React app (v3.5)
- [x] S1.1 · React API on the Preact runtime — [PR #2](https://github.com/Gualbertol123/slidemaster/pull/2). Vite/Vitest alias `react`, `react-dom`, `react-dom/client`, `react/jsx-runtime` → `preact/compat`; `jsxImportSource: "react"` with `@types/react` 19.2; an AST codemod renamed ~590 JSX attributes (template-string HTML keeps `class=`). New `ui/Input.tsx`: React's `onChange` fires per input and its controlled inputs restore the value after every event, which would break the 3 colour pickers (they commit on the native `change`, now `onCommit`), the 5 sliders and the wizard's boxes that store text without re-rendering. E15 also fails on console errors/warnings (4 fixture-caused browser messages allowed; a probe `console.warn` fails it). Page 464,826 → 473,244 bytes (gzip 153,654 → 155,956, +1.5 %). Review found: JSX runtime aliased to `preact/jsx-runtime` (compat mappings only active by import accident) → `preact/compat/jsx-runtime`; wizard version radio without handler → `readOnly`; Ribbon `tb()` spread `class`/string `style` (untyped, missed by tsc) → fixed. Parity 0 differences (full comparison), twice. verified: tests ✓ clean clone ✓ review ✓
- [x] S1.2 · Repository layout — [PR #2](https://github.com/Gualbertol123/slidemaster/pull/2). `git mv frontend app` (history follows); root npm workspaces `app`, `core` (one lockfile, `node_modules/` at the root, root scripts `build`/`check`/`test:e2e`/`parity`); `core/` empty, `lib: ["ES2022","WebWorker"]`, `types: []` (a `document` probe fails TS2584), own CI job `core`. The page is byte-identical (sha256 2d58fd7c0b29…). Paths fixed in CI, release.yml, tools, spikes, README §2/§7.5/§11/§16, docs, the release checklist, helper messages and tests. Surprises: `.gitignore` ignored `/app/` (side-by-side installs `app\<ver>\`, `app\current.json`): now only those are ignored, the sources sit next to them; `tools/update.py` `clear_leftovers` now removes only version-named `.old-*`/`.part` folders (test added). Review found the regenerated lockfile had bumped Playwright 1.63→1.64 (another Chromium), rollup and @types/react: rebuilt from the old lock, 0 version changes. verified: tests ✓ clean clone ✓ review ✓
- [x] S1.3 · Store with selectors replaces the global S — [PR #2](https://github.com/Gualbertol123/slidemaster/pull/2). One zustand 5.0.15 store (`subscribeWithSelector`) in slices doc / deck / selection / ui / prefs (`state/store.ts`); `S`, `emit()`, `useApp()` gone (the `S.` left in `issues.ts`/`preset.ts` are local sheets); components read through selectors (`useShallow` for objects). Render counts (`npm run bench`, Testing Library + Profiler on React 19, jsdom): selecting or extending a cell selection re-renders 4 components (FxBar, Ribbon, Status, TableRibbon; the stage overlay is imperative and follows `selection` through `followStore`), 10 polls with nothing new 0 (offline and with equal helper answers), a toast only Toast. Deviations, deliberate: the actions stay module functions over `get()`/`patch()` (the store holds data only; ADR-012's "store actions" in name); `thumbs.ts` subscribes to nothing (thumbnails are redrawn by the document changes that know which slide changed, as in v3); the stage follows `selection` and `ui.zoom` only, slides are redrawn by `state/app.ts`; PLAN's `selection.cells` is `selection.sel`; `ui/Input.tsx` attaches its `change` listener in a layout effect (its comment: a passive effect can run a frame later, and a `change` before it would be lost; no failing case was recorded for it). The render counts are measured on React 19 while the page still runs Preact until S1.4. Review (independent sub-agent) found no data-flow bug; fixed: **Fit** pressed while already fitted no longer re-fitted (`setZoom` re-fits when unchanged; test), poll test now also covers a served helper's equal-but-new answers, stage subscription unit-tested (`tests/stage-follow.test.tsx`); both new tests fail with the fix removed. Four screen updates now come at once instead of with the next poll (Decisions). Run on the corporate Windows PC (Node 22.23.3, Python 3.12.7): check 128 tests, helper 181 OK, e2e 15 passed, parity 0 differences (text-only: not the goldens' Chromium/fonts); CI green on PR #2. - [x] S1.4 · Switch to real React 19 — [PR #2](https://github.com/Gualbertol123/slidemaster/pull/2). Aliases and `preact` removed; `react`/`react-dom` 19.2.8 exact in `dependencies` (the lock changes only by preact gone and dev flags cleared; no version changed); `createRoot` as before; `<StrictMode>` only under `import.meta.env.DEV` (`npm run dev`; `boot()` runs outside React, every component effect cleans up). Every raw `<input>`/`<select>` traced: their `onChange` updates the shown value synchronously (local state or a synchronous op → store), so React's controlled-input restore loses nothing; `ui/Input.tsx` (uncontrolled) and `ui/Field.tsx` (draft while focused, E8) unchanged. **React Compiler: tried, not used** (`@vitejs/plugin-react` 5.2.0 + `babel-plugin-react-compiler` 1.0.0, also in the component tests): render counts unchanged, but e2e E2 failed and parity found 48 differences (the side panel's issues text): components read objects that the app changes in place (e.g. `R.cfg`), which the compiler's caches assume never happens; reverted, nothing of it left. "No React warning": the e2e page is React's production build, which prints none, so the component tests (React's development build) now fail on any console error/warning (`tests/setup-console.ts`; it fails on the old `Dialog`); it found one: `ui/Dialogs.tsx` keyed the button instead of its fragment (fixed). New `e2e/file.spec.ts`: the built page from file:// (no helper), a fixture through `#fileInput`, the wizard, a cell edit kept in localStorage across a reload. Review (independent sub-agent): no bug in the switch; findings fixed above, plus a README nit; one older CommentDialog typing issue → Waiting for. Run on the corporate Windows PC: check 128 tests, helper 181 OK, e2e 16 passed, parity 0 differences (text-only), render counts unchanged (4 · 4 · 0 · 0 · 1). ### M2 · The core in workers (v3.6)
- [x] S2.1 · Move pure code into core/ — [PR #5](https://github.com/Gualbertol123/slidemaster/pull/5). `git mv` of `app/src/xlsx`, `app/src/model` and the pure parts of `app/src/render` into `core/src` (33 files moved unchanged). The 7 pure unit tests (incl. `shared/ops-vectors.json`) run in core (Vitest, node; CI job `core` = `npm run check -w core`); `backend/tests/test_upgrade.py` reads FORMATS from `core/src/model/types.ts`. Without a platform the core behaves as v3 outside a page (digit width 7 px, no picture analysis). Lockfile: only the two workspace entries changed (a regenerated lock would have added 53 `peer` flags, no version change: not taken). - [x] S2.2 · XML tokenizer instead of DOMParser — [PR #5](https://github.com/Gualbertol123/slidemaster/pull/5). `core/src/xlsx/xml.ts`: a pull tokenizer (elements, attributes, text, CDATA, character and predefined entities, namespace prefixes; comments, PIs and a DOCTYPE skipped; CRLF/CR → LF and tab/line ends in attribute values → spaces, as an XML parser must) and a small tree with DOM semantics behind `platform.ts`'s `XmlElement` (`getAttributeNS` with prefix scopes, `getElementsByTagNameNS` in document order, iterative walks). The readers of rels, workbook, theme, styles (gradient fills, dxfs) and drawings run on it unchanged; the page no longer installs DOMParser (`platform().xml` stays as the equivalence test's hook only). `core/tests/xml.test.ts` (jsdom in that test only; `core/tests/tsconfig.json` adds Node + DOM types for tests while `core/src` stays ES2022 + WebWorker): the JSON of everything those readers produce is identical with DOMParser for every corpus workbook (big30 too when generated) and for a synthetic workbook with what the corpus lacks (`tests/zipbuild.ts`: gradient fills, a custom theme with sysClr, an `x:`-prefixed styles part with BOM and CRLF, dxfs, `mc:AlternateContent`, a chart frame, one-cell and absolute anchors, `r:embed` declared on a nested element), its variants with an empty rels/styles/theme/drawing/workbook part, and crafted XML details; mutations (no attribute decoding, no text entities, no namespaces) make it fail. Malformed XML is read leniently where DOMParser gave an error document (an empty part reads as an empty document, as before); DOCTYPE entities are not expanded (OOXML has none). Results, both runs (working copy, then clean clone; after the review fixes again both): check 134 core (incl. big30) + 23 app, helper 181 OK, e2e 16 passed (1.5–1.6 min), parity 0 differences (text-only on this PC; jsdom is the equivalence reference, Chromium only through parity), render counts 4 · 4 · 0 · 0 · 1. - [x] S2.3 · Columnar SheetStore and byte scanner — [PR #5](https://github.com/Gualbertol123/slidemaster/pull/5). fflate 0.8.2 (pinned) replaces JSZip (`core/src/xlsx/zip.ts`: central directory, parts inflated on demand, in slices with progress when the page reads; a wrong size in the directory, names without the UTF-8 flag and duplicate names behave as with JSZip). `core/src/xlsx/sheetstore.ts`: one pass over `sheetData`'s bytes into typed-array columns (column, kind, xf, number or string index: 13 bytes a cell; strings interned by their bytes), covering every cell shape v3's regex parser read; `cells`/`rowInfo` become read-only map views and a v3 `Cell` is made on request, so buildLayout, findRegions, evalCF, formatValue, the detector and the comments run unchanged; the rest of a sheet is read with v3's regular expressions. `core/tests/sheetstore.test.ts` compares it with v3's parser (kept verbatim in `tests/sheet-v3.ts`) cell by cell on every corpus sheet incl. big30 and on crafted sheets; the reviewer's differential fuzzer (≈ 200 000 random sheets, 300 000 numbers) finds 0 differences. ⏱ `npm run bench -w core` (CI job `bench` generates big30 with openpyxl, a dev tool): a big30 sheet (130 000 rows × 12, 75.7 MB of XML) parsed — inflated XML → Sheet, timed directly — in under 0.5 s and under 40 MB retained; inflate ≈ 0.2–0.3 s is reported apart (06-plan B-2: inflate + parse), the whole read in the page is S2.6's gate. - [x] S2.4 · Core worker, typed RPC, the document moves into the worker — [PR #5](https://github.com/Gualbertol123/slidemaster/pull/5). The core worker (`core/src/workers/core.worker.ts`, host `core/src/worker/host.ts`) OWNS the open workbook, its shared document (DocSync moved to `core/src/sync/`, still v3's HTTP ops protocol) and undo/redo (`core/src/sync/history.ts`: labels, 1.5 s coalescing, 200 steps, cleared when a workbook is opened as in v3). Typed RPC `core/src/rpc/{port,messages}.ts` (calls both ways, events, transferables); the page's end `app/src/rpc/client.ts`; `app/vite.config.ts` bundles the worker into the one file as a string started from a Blob URL (classic: `file://` works; esbuild 0.28.2, the version vite already used, declared). The page holds read-only mirrors: sheet slices (`core/src/xlsx/slice.ts`: whole when small, else the rectangle the deck's tables, grown ranges and conditional-format operands need; `mirror.ts`), on which it keeps running the pure functions; `doc.view` (change() shows ops at once, predicted with core's ops, rebased on the worker's snapshots by an acknowledgement count); `deck.history`. After a change the worker sends slices, then the table HTML (`RenderCtx.htmlOf`; `tableHtmlKey` now includes the layout's geometry: per-design sizes were not in `tableKey`), then the snapshot. The worker asks the page for the helper's API (also the offline backend of `file://`), Excel's digit width with the page's fonts, picture analysis (no SVG decoding in workers) and EMF conversion. New tests: `core/tests/host.test.ts` (open, slices → HTML → snapshot, acks, undo/redo, coalescing, two people, per-design sizes, a second workbook, HTML after undo), `slice.test.ts` (every corpus deck gives the same slides, layouts, items and HTML from slices, whole and cut to rectangles; CF operands outside the table), `docsync.test.ts` moved; `app/e2e/file.spec.ts` now checks "Saved in this browser only", undo/redo, and that the core runs in a worker with 0 tables drawn by the page. Reviews: e2e found stale HTML after "Same size" (fixed by the key above) and "✓ Saved" shown before the worker had the change (the save state is now optimistic, as DocSync's was); the independent sub-agent: REQUEST CHANGES → fixed: calls during the opening of a second workbook read the open one (workbooks are now numbered, slices and calls carry the number), the history was not cleared on open, slice rectangles were remembered across workbooks by sheet name, the HTML handover never resent after undo (now the last key per table), Ctrl+Z with nothing to undo flashed "Unsaved changes…"; nits fixed (a failing apply drops the page's prediction, no change/undo while another workbook starts, export asks the worker once per design and version, the worker resolves other designs as the page does, version/default-style redraws wait for the worker's HTML, watch paths). Known, not fixed here: the wizard still gets whole sheets (S2.5 replaces that), a dead worker leaves calls waiting. Results, both runs (working copy, clean clone): check 203 core + 14 app, helper 181 OK, e2e 16 passed, parity 0 differences (the clean clone's first parity run hit a TCP timeout to the capture's helper before comparing; rerun: 0), render counts 4 · 4 · 0 · 0 · 1, bench parse 353–405 ms, 32–33 MB. Page 728,206 bytes (gzip -9 246,129; +23 %: core is in the page and in the worker). verified: tests ✓ clean clone ✓ review ✓
- [x] S2.5 · Wizard and formula bar read cells through the worker — [PR #5](https://github.com/Gualbertol123/slidemaster/pull/5). The wizard no longer receives sheets: `getSheetMeta` (title, "x" regions, used range; rows × columns of table definitions), `getSheetWindow` (the cells of the preview as it draws them: text as shown, fill, colour, bold, alignment, row/column px and hidden), `detectTables` and `getCell` (the formula bar) run in the worker (`core/src/xlsx/sheetview.ts`; the detector moved to `core/src/xlsx/detect.ts`); a few hundred rows travel, never a sheet. The preview grid is drawn in blocks of 40 rows, one per frame (a whole 400-row preview in one task measured ~60 ms; two blocks in one frame ~74 ms). Review findings, fixed: the formula bar's cache key lacked the workbook number (a reloaded workbook showed the old value), the wizard's steps could be clicked before its first answer, a failed window left "Reading…" for ever (now "could not be shown: …"), a range past the preview was boxed at the last drawn cell while blocks were still coming, and `tableSize` went through `resolveTable`, which caches and attaches ids to the layouts the worker draws the deck with (now computed without touching them; `core/tests/host.test.ts` compares it with `resolveTable`). Long tasks (Long Tasks API, big30: index → wizard, then the 130k-row sheet → grid), 12 runs: none over 50 ms in either phase. Earlier rare 60–90 ms tasks were traced to 1,547 Chromium processes leaked by the parity helper on Windows (`tools/capture-v3.mjs` killed the helper, not its export browser; fixed in S2.6), not to the page. Results, both runs (working copy, clean clone): check 205 core + 14 app, helper 181 OK, e2e 16 passed, parity 0 differences, bench parse 323–373 ms, 32–33 MB. Page 734,793 bytes (gzip -9 248,399), identical in the clean clone. verified: tests ✓ clean clone ✓ review ✓
- [x] S2.6 · Smoothness gate for opening — [PR #5](https://github.com/Gualbertol123/slidemaster/pull/5). `tools/bench.mjs` (from `spikes/baseline/measure.mjs`): the committed page with a real helper in Chromium, two runs, the worse counts, +10 % slack; times taken in the page (click → element there, a MutationObserver: Playwright's waits poll at up to 500 ms and had added that much to some runs); part of `npm run bench` and of CI's bench job (Playwright Chromium, results uploaded as `bench-results.json`); `--page DIR` measures another build (v3). To get there: the worker and the page are built for es2022 (lowered to es2020, class fields became defineProperty calls and the sheet scanner ran 3× slower in the browser: ~1 s instead of ~0.35 s); a sheet is inflated in one call into its final buffer (fflate's streaming inflater cost 150–300 ms more in the worker); a long read yields at most every 200 ms instead of a timer per 4000 rows and per sheet (`pacer`; ~4 ms per timer in a worker: deck20's 20 sheets waited ~65 ms); the detector and the used range read the columns (`core/src/xlsx/detect.ts`, `core/tests/detect.test.ts` keeps v3's code as the reference, corpus + crafted cases); the page's mirror lays out marker tables when first read and while idle during the wizard, and the default preset comes from the worker (opening deck20 had laid out all 20 sheets' tables twice). `tools/capture-v3.mjs` stops the helper's whole process tree on Windows (its export browsers were left running: ~1,500 after a day of parity runs, which also skewed timings). Decision 1.a (2026-10-11, below): the tasks from Finish until the first slide is drawn are reported, not gated (Chromium's first use of each Segoe UI Variable instance on Windows, ~6 ms each; v3 had a 112–154 ms task there too); pre-warming the fonts was tried and dropped (not reliable in the page, and it slowed other phases). Review findings, fixed: the worker's `defaultPreset` now takes the workbook's number (two opens racing could get the other's preset; `host.test.ts`), the idle layout stops when the workbook is no longer the one opening or open. - [x] S4.2 · Smooth drags — branch `v4/M4-stage` (503564e, 3a217b5; the PR is not opened yet, see Waiting for). `computeLayout` and the per-design sizing (`sizingOf` / `sizingPatch` / `sizingChange`, `fixedScale`, notes) moved verbatim to `core/src/render/layout.ts`, pure, shared by the worker and the page (README gotcha 11). Every drag previews from store state at animation-frame rate on the UI thread (the corner's bisection solved once per frame and drawn in that frame); pointer-up sends one change (one undo step); a cancelled pointer changes nothing. Each table is now `<div class="tw" data-i>` holding its `<svg>`: Chromium lays an svg's text out again whenever the scale of the svg element's own transform changes (will-change or not; 117 ms vs 3.5 ms for 30 frames in a probe), never for an ancestor's, so a resize scales the div (will-change while the pointer is on the corner handle and during the drag). A text box being stretched shows its live text until the slide drawn with the new size arrives. New e2e: corner scale, grip move, border double-click, each one undo step; the soft-shadows check counts blurs in `.slide svg` (was `.slide > svg`: table SVGs now sit in their div; mapping in Waiting for). ⏱ `npm run bench` drag frames (the page's main-thread work between two animation frames, from a trace; outermost tasks only – nested `ThreadControllerImpl::RunTask` had been counted twice, which read 33.5 ms for the corner): border 12–13, corner 8.7–14.2, stretch 6–8, grip 5–7, note move 6–9, note stretch 6–9 ms (limit 16). Gaps between animation frames are reported too: ≤ 17.6 ms for every drag except the browser's first corner drag (83–100 ms, then 33 ms, once per browser: the GPU process's first scaled draw – a 97–104 ms `GPUTask` in SwiftShader, the renderer's main thread idle; a second bench run in the same browser shows ≤ 17.4 ms). Review (independent sub-agent): approve after fixes – the stretch drop showed the old wrap for ~35 ms (fixed, re-measured: live text until the new layer), the resize preview was a frame late, `pointercancel` committed, the armed state could stick after a drop (fixed), TextLayer's memo compared JSON first (fixed), README still said `svg.tw` (fixed); the GPU gap (above; not the page's work); HBox / Notes / Selection re-render per drag frame but stay under 8 ms in all (not changed). - [x] S4.3 · Thumbnails as bitmaps — branch `v4/M4-stage` (126dd25, 801270b; the PR is not opened yet, see Waiting for). B14: the thumbnails are pictures of the slides – an export worker asks the core worker for the slides' display lists on its own channel and draws them as the PNG export does (OffscreenCanvas → ImageBitmap; the library's fonts and the pictures as for exports), the page puts each into a `bitmaprenderer` canvas, never laying a slide out on its thread; big tables show their text (no "shapes only"). A slide is drawn again only when its list changed: the worker keys each picture (nodes, picture ids, width, the library's faces – `core/src/dl/key.ts`, shared with the stage) and leaves out pictures the page shows; one drawn without a picture or a face it needs gets no key and is drawn again next time. Deviations, deliberate: **"virtualised" = every row stays in the list** (number, title, tag, badge, `data-i`: 507 nodes for 100 slides, no React commit while scrolling), only frames near the view hold a picture (asked for 300 px ahead, pixels freed 1500 px away: 7–13 pictures in memory); **thumbnails use the export pool** (an export running meanwhile delays them to 0.4–1 s and may start a second worker; their lists queue behind an export's in the core) – both reviewed and kept. The core worker no longer builds the shapes-only table HTML. ⏱ bench (new): deck20's content slides five times (100 slides), the list scrolled down and up with the wheel, traced: 60 fps, longest gap between animation frames 17.5–17.8 ms (gate 20 ms: one missed frame is 33 ms); the page's most work in a frame 12.8–16.4 ms is one major garbage collection, every other frame ≤ 7 ms (reported, not gated). Keypress p95 fell from ~41 to 26–39 ms (thumbnails no longer lay slides out on the page). Review (independent sub-agent): no blockers, 60 fps confirmed on its own trace (538 frames, longest gap 17.0 ms, longest task 6.2 ms, pictures matching the stage in the three designs); fixed: a failed request left its frames blank (retried after 2 s), a picture or face that failed once was locked in by its key, frames that left the list stayed observed, a change of pixel ratio was ignored, the dead shapes-only HTML; recorded above: the export pool and the reading of "virtualised". e2e: a new test (pictures, cover / index tags, an edit draws its own slide again and no other). Not run: W13 of the step's "Done when" (two PCs with different fonts give identical layouts) is a field test that needs people and their PCs (Waiting for, with G0). verified: tests ✓ clean clone ✓ review ✓. First committed as "S4.4 · Measurement from font files (pending sign-off)": the core worker measures the display list's text with HarfBuzz on the font files (`core/src/text/measure.ts`: the library's faces and this PC's – the helper's /sysfonts, from disk the browser's local fonts only when already given; the page measures what has no readable file, or a text with a character its face lacks); a Windows font's face is the one Chromium draws (DirectWrite's nearest weight, not CSS matching – Segoe UI 500 is Semibold, 720 Bold – in the measurer, the PDF's resolver and the registry, so the PDF takes the screen's faces); ascent / descent as Chromium rounds them; the legacy 'kern' table where GPOS has no kern feature (`kern.ts`; harfbuzzjs's lean build leaves it out). On the reference PC every corpus font measures as Chromium's canvas exactly (Calibri, Century Gothic, Segoe UI all weights, Cambria, Consolas, Verdana); Tahoma, Times New Roman and Arial differ on some pairs (≤ 1.4 %). Excel's digit width from the file. Liquid Glass: static Segoe UI on screen (B2); columns widened by measured widths (glass.ts `glassWidth`, through `platform().text.width`, which the page answers with its canvas and the worker with HarfBuzz – the same widths – the worker loading the glass faces first); text boxes fitted on their own layout in slide-dl (every size in one measuring round; the page's DOM fitting and the `fitNotes` RPC are gone). Per-font check (05 §3.4): a face whose browser widths differ from HarfBuzz's by more than 0.5 % on a probe text is drawn as glyph outlines on the stage (`outline.ts`: hb_font_draw_glyph through hb.wasm's function table; `svg.ts` `outline`: each glyph defined once, used at its pen); the check runs half a second after the first draw and sends the slide again if it changes anything. Parity: Excel and Excel Refined unchanged (no geometry difference); Liquid Glass 22,298 differences – font lists (B2) and positions (glass tables 1.5–15 % narrower, median 4.4 %; text boxes follow; 2 PDF line breaks; 2 screenshots) – as entries in `accepted.json` (one per slide and kind, for parity and for dlparity), signed "pending: S4.4 sign-off (review/S4.4/)". SP: 85 of 135 slides beyond 1 % (glyph rasterisation): drawing every font as outlines makes it worse (deck20 Excel 2.79 → 2.90 %, glass 2.08 → 2.74 %), so SP cannot pass as 05 §3.4 states it – the question is in `review/S4.4/README.md`. Found on the way: an outline's scale written with 3 decimals drew text 2.4 % too wide (fixed, test); the per-font check's page probes cost the first draw 35 ms (moved after it); the first measurements waited for the font files (the common faces are read at worker start). ⏱ bench: deck20 open 370–373 ms, keypress p95 25–26 ms, drag frames ≤ 11.7 ms, thumbnails 60 fps. New tools: `tools/s44-package.mjs` (the review package), `parity.mjs` / `dlparity.mjs --diffs`, `SB_OUTLINES=all` for capture (the outlines switch). Review (independent sub-agent): REQUEST CHANGES, 3 high – (H1) a family the catalog has but cannot read (every Google font is WOFF2) was measured with the next family of its list, its text stretched by `textLength` and, through the per-font check, drawn as that family's outlines; (H2) the check's re-push changed no key, so a table drawn before a font was flagged kept its text for the session; (H3) the stage's first frame waited for /sysfonts and font files without a limit (a 25 s helper delay held it 50–77 s: the likely cause of the one parity capture that timed out) – and medium: (M1) the page's width() measured fonts the worker could not (from disk without local fonts: glass tables up to 15 % wider than the page believed, clicks off their cells), (M2) a failed catalog was kept for the session, (M4) weight ties below 400 go lighter, (M5) entries matched any difference of their slide. Fixed: such a family is measured by the page; the faces drawn as outlines are part of the stage's keys; measuring waits 1.5 s at most for a font (the page measures meanwhile, the slide is drawn again when it comes; library font fetches time out after 20 s); the page's width() answers only for fonts the worker reads (`core/src/text/family.ts`, shared); a failed catalog is asked for again and a library change measures again; ties below 400 lighter; tabular runs as outlines at the list's advances; kern subtables bounded; each pending entry carries a digest of its differences (`parity.mjs` accepts its scope only while they are the same). Re-measured with the reviewer's scripts: no outlines of another family, the Tahoma tables outlines on every visit, first slide in 0.4–1.5 s with /sysfonts 25 s late (12 opens). Kept: the HTML paths' glass geometry (sendHtml, the old engine – S4.5 deletes them); fonts held twice in the worker (file and wasm heap; deck fonts only). The clean clone of 804bdd9 found dlparity's differences not listed (its entries carry tool "dlparity"): now listed as well, pending the same sign-off. **Signed off 2026-10-14** (Decisions): the entries are signed; SP is option (a), in `tools/stage-capture.mjs` `compareSP` – every display-list run starts where the PDF's characters do (PDFium's origins, `pdfcheck.py chars`, 0.05 pt; a run with a text-shadow copy may match at the copy's origin, as PDFium drops one of two overlapping characters), every run has its measured width in the browser (a `<text>` without textLength in its font: 0.5 %, 2 % for a run drawn as outlines, tabular runs by their advances without kerning; `TextNode.ls` gives the letter-spacing included in w), the pixels at ΔE 2 / 1 % with only the glyph pixels inside each run's box left out (what lies under the text is compared) and each box's ink (the mean of its glyph pixels on both sides) within 140 of the PDF's. Its first version, whole-box masks, was reviewed again (independent sub-agent, REQUEST CHANGES): (1) every measuring call waited the 1.5 s budget again for a font still pending (3–4 s per slide switch for up to a minute when /sysfonts is slow) – a font now waits once; (2) the page's font gate kept its answers when the catalog changed – cleared; (3) SP could not see a run measured with the wrong face once its font was drawn as outlines, (4) most glass runs (tabular) had no width check, (5) whole-box masks hid fills and lines under text, (6) a failed /sysfonts list reached the worker as "no fonts" – all fixed as above; lows fixed: a missing text file is a difference, the SP summary counts its kinds apart, the S4.4 pixel entries carry a cap (`upTo`, %). The colour check found that the stage draws a faint light seam between two dark cells of a scaled table where the PDF has none – v3 drew it too (GP against v3's goldens fails when it is removed), so it stays and is under Waiting for. SP pixels beyond 1 % remain on two Liquid Glass slides (weekly-comments 1.02 %, weekly-looks 1.18 %): hairlines – the thin row separators and capsule edges, anti-aliased differently by Chromium and PDFium – accepted under the S4.1 sign-off ("glyph rasterisation and hairlines") with caps. The intermittent capture timeout was caught by the new diagnostics in `capture-v3.mjs` `load()`: the helper stopped answering for a while after the capture's old-engine PDF exports (S3.7 had seen it), the page could not read the workbook ("No workbook") – the old engine goes in S4.5. Third review round (REQUEST CHANGES, one item): with glyph pixels left out wherever either side had ink, a run missing on one side or a line drawn under the text on one side only went unseen (the reviewer's controls: a title erased, a 2 px line through every box) – each box's glyph pixel count is now compared too (screen against PDF within 0.25–4 when either has 40: the two rasterisers' glyph weights alone move it by up to 2.7× on the corpus): an erased title (0 against 9,253) is caught, the untampered corpus has no difference – a 2 px line under the text on one side only stays within the rasterisers' spread and is not seen (named in stage-capture.mjs); its follow-ups done: faces looked for while the catalog could not be read are forgotten (test), and once the catalog missed a budget no further measurement waits for it. Its measured controls: SP catches a font measured with another face's file (sp-width on 40 of 40 slides), the ink check catches text recoloured by ~150 RGB or more.

### M6 · Hardening (v4.1)
- [x] S6.1 · Native .xlsb reader — [PR (to open)](https://github.com/Gualbertol123/slidemaster/compare/main...v4/M6-hardening), branch `v4/M6-hardening`; 92c4949, b4a4450. `core/src/xlsx/xlsb/`: a BIFF12 record reader (1–2-byte type, 1–4-byte size) for workbook.bin (sheets, hidden / very hidden), styles.bin (BrtFmt, BrtFont, BrtFill incl. gradients, BrtBorder, BrtXF, BrtDXF), sharedStrings.bin (rich runs: a run is raised / lowered by its font's sss) and the sheets (BrtRowHdr, every BrtCell* / BrtShort* / BrtFmla* record, BrtWsFmtInfo, BrtColInfo, merges, conditional formats with their formulas decompiled to the .xlsx text) into the SAME SheetStore columns (`ColumnsBuilder` in sheetstore.ts) and Sheet facade; rels, theme and drawings stay XML and use the existing readers. Every value is mapped to what the .xlsx reader makes of the XML Excel writes for the same workbook. **Surprises** (each found by a twin, then followed): Excel writes a line feed into the XML as CR LF (and CR as `_x000D_`), which the .xlsx reader keeps, so binary text gets CR LF too; an empty formula result is `<v/>` = a blank cell; relative CF references are offsets (modulo the sheet) from the top-left of ALL the rule's ranges, not its first range; the .xlsx reader also takes x14 extension copies of rules as range-less rules (they never apply; the binary reader skips them); the .xlsx reader looks for `baseColumnWidth`, an attribute Excel never writes (it writes `baseColWidth`), so a base width alone is ignored – the binary reader does the same (tech debt, Waiting for); Italian Excel takes FormatConditions formulas and unions of ranges in its own language (the twin script translates through FormulaLocal and the list separator). Excel COM stays for .xls and protected files only (the page's XLSB branch removed). Twins: `tools/make-xlsb-twins.ps1` saves every corpus workbook from this PC's Excel 16 as .xlsb AND as Excel's own .xlsx (the corpus originals are openpyxl's, which Excel normalises) in `tests/corpus/workbooks/xlsb/`, plus `edge-xlsb` built by Excel (errors, booleans, inline / formula / empty strings, every border style, a gradient, alignments, row and column formats, italic + bold CF, rules over two ranges, functions, data bar, unique values, contains text, hidden and very hidden sheets). `core/tests/xlsb.test.ts`: the 10 twins read the same cell by cell (values, types, formats, cell formats, rows, columns, merges, CF, drawings, titles, table regions, colPx / rowPx); all 14 corpus decks give the same slides, items, glass geometry, display lists and notes in the 3 designs and every version, also through the page's slices; crafted records (types / sizes, RK, bounds, short cells, an unreadable CF formula, the default width, a cut-short record). Against the openpyxl originals: values and types equal; formatted text equal except formula cells openpyxl saved without results (Excel computed them). big30 saved by Excel as .xlsb (20.6 MB) and .xlsx: the 4.68 M cells equal; one 130k-row sheet read in Node 668–884 ms (.xlsb) vs 866–891 ms (.xlsx). e2e: an .xlsb opens from file:// (no helper, no Excel) – fails on 4.0's page. W6 (Constrained Language Mode) itself is a field check (field-results §3): the .xlsb path no longer uses PowerShell. Run 1 (working copy 92c4949): build identical, check 457 + 41, helper 228 OK on Python 3.12 and 3.8, e2e 24, parity 0 not accepted (text-only, as in M5), load PASS ×5, bench within every limit at ×1.04 (deck20 open 409 ms – limit 414, the M6 item below; big30 read 729, longest task 0, keypress p95 34, drag 13.1, thumbs gap 17.3). Run 2 (clean clones, %TEMP%\verify-S6.1*): 92c4949 build identical, check 457 + 41, helper 228 ×2, e2e 23 + 1 failed (E15: `fonts.gstatic.com` refused by CORS at that moment – the network, not the change), parity 0, load PASS ×5, bench ×1.00 (deck20 open 386, big30 849, keypress 34, drag 13.4, thumbs 17); b4a4450 build identical, check 460 + 41, helper 228 ×2, e2e 24, parity 0, load PASS ×5, bench within every limit at ×1.16 (deck20 open 399, big30 782, longest task 0, keypress 36, drag 13.7, thumbs 17.4). big30 read 729 / 849 / 782 ms differ by up to 16 %: the .xlsx path, which this step only refactored (finishSheet), on a PC loaded by the other runs; calibration ×1.00–1.16. Review: REQUEST CHANGES (a "not equal" rule with an unreadable formula matched every cell; BrtShort* cells dropped; the default width when only baseColWidth is set; stale docs) → fixed in b4a4450 with tests, plus a CF over 200k ranges (stack overflow) and a cut-short record losing only itself → APPROVE (fuzzing: 480 mutated files, none hung or crashed; a 1M-row .xlsb in 857 ms in the worker path). verified: tests ✓ clean clone ✓ review ✓

- [x] 4.10.1 · .xlsb that failed with "a string is longer than its record" (owner's report 2026-10-22, "Dashboard.xlsb",
  Sheets step; branch `v4/xlsb-string-fix` on main a6d5ce3) — (commit: see the branch; runs: by the orchestrator). Root
  cause: BrtDXF's XFProp 0x26 (xfPropNumFmt) is an XLWideString filling the property (MS-XLSB 2.5.162; the id is the
  separate 0x29, as LibreOffice reads it); S16.4 (4.9) read a 2-byte id before it, so the length read took the string's
  first character as its high half and styles.bin – read with no recovery when the sheets are chosen – failed for every
  workbook with a rule or table style setting a number format. Also: BrtSupBookSrc's RelID is nullable; an unreadable
  BrtName / BrtSupBookSrc / BrtBundleSh no longer shifts the numbers of the others; a failed BrtBeginCFRule no longer
  leaves the previous rule to take the next colour scale's parts. Resilience: styles, rules, cells and other sheet
  records that cannot be read are skipped with a default in their place and named (`Sheet.notRead`, carried by the
  slice, one Issues line each); the sheet list and shared strings name the part, item and record. Tests: core
  `xlsb.test.ts` "4.10.1 ·" (crafted records + every corpus .xlsb reads with nothing skipped), app `item-text.test.ts`;
  page budget `page-budget/x4101.json` (+9,300 by measurement). Versions 4.10.1 (CHANGELOG dated 2026-10-22).

## Decisions

(Human decisions the plan needs, e.g. gate G0 outcomes. An agent never writes here on its own.)

- 2026-10-20 · **CI repeats a timing gate before failing it** (product owner: "make a prompt for me to fix this" – the
  go-ahead for the rule the prompt proposed; recorded by the agent). The last three merges to main (#21, #22, #23) failed
  only the bench, each on a different timing gate, from stalls of GitHub's runner (#23: one ~380 ms stall counted by
  three gates); AUDIT-2 PERF-4. **On CI only**, a timing gate (ms) fails only when it is over its limit + 10 % in **two
  separate measurements**: when the first pass (the bench's two runs, the worse counting) is over, the bench measures
  that scenario again in a fresh page (the core benches: again in the process, the engine's cold first use in a fresh
  one) and fails only if the repeat is over too. Every limit and every scaling stays as it is (PLAN Part F unchanged).
  Locally nothing changes (one pass, the worse run counts). Size gates (KB, MB) and render counts are never repeated.
  The report prints both measurements and "repeated after a stall" for a gate that passed only on the repeat, and keeps
  the `::error` line for a gate over in both. One function for every gate: `tools/benchgate.mjs` (README §11.3).
- 2026-10-20 · **B43: Scan for "x" tables in the wizard** (product owner: "big files that do not get scanned internally
  when selecting the sheets to examine. Please add a force to scan button so that X's can be scanned inside the tables
  to already identify the tables"; recorded by the agent). A large workbook (over 12 MB of sheet XML or a 6 MB file)
  reads a sheet only when it is selected, so step 1 showed "not read yet" and no "x" tables until *Next*. Step 1 of a
  large workbook gets **Scan for "x" tables (n)**: the selected sheets not read yet are read at once – the same read
  *Next* does, so no new memory risk – and show their "x" tables, title and suggestions; their "x" tables are defined
  as *Next* would define them. Sheets not selected stay unread (to scan everything: *All*, then *Scan*). PLAN Part B
  §8 B43, released with M13 (4.6.0).
- 2026-10-20 · **B44: delete saved setups** (product owner: "i also want to be able to delete setups for documents";
  then, asked by the agent, three answers: (1) **"Start over, keep a backup"** – the whole saved deck goes, kept as a
  backup an administrator can restore; (2) offered **in the "Continue with preset" box and in ⚙ Options ›
  Administration**; (3) **refused while others have it open**). A deck is moved – in one rename, all or nothing –
  with its snapshot backups and its frozen v3 file to `data/v4/deleted/<key>~<ms>/` (helper `deckbin.py`), under the
  `wb-<key>` lock and the deck lock; another window's presence refuses it (the asking window's own does not; its own
  open deck is not offered); a deck given back to v3 or a document v3 owns is never deleted. In its place, under the
  same locks, an **empty deck with gen + 1 and a creation id of its own** (the file `v4/decks/<key>/born`, which no
  compaction of any version touches; a deck no delete created has none and behaves as before) and a frozen v3 stub: a window
  whose presence lapsed (a sleeping PC) can neither compact over it with the old gen nor open a journal in it with a
  writer of the old deck (writers are bound to `born` at `/api/decks/open`, cached 10 s; refused with "reload" –
  while the helper that issued the writer runs and is 4.6 or later: the release rule closes every window on update
  day); no append re-creates a deleted folder. (Left behind, the frozen v3 file would have been thawed and the old
  setup brought back; the backups would have made the open refuse the deck: `decks.traces`.) *Saved setups…* lists
  the setups and the deleted ones; *Restore…* works – all or nothing – while the workbook's deck is empty (not for
  the deck this window has open). Two reviews: REQUEST CHANGES (11 findings), then REQUEST CHANGES (6: `born` moved
  out of the snapshot – helpers before 4.6 rewrite it without the key – into its own file, cached; Restore not for the
  open deck; tests for decks without `born`, a helper restart, lean-lock); all fixed but the note that the deleted
  setup's linked workbooks stay listed in the new wizard. PLAN Part B §8 B44, M13 (4.6.0).

- 2026-10-19 · **PowerPoint: slide masters generated, standardised and lean – no bulk pictures, no connections, tables**
  (product owner, in the session that starts M11: "auto generate slidemasters as well for consistency on export,
  everything has to be standardised and lean. no bulk images and connections on slides, use tables etc"; recorded by
  the agent, with how it reads it). This amends 07 §4.1–§4.2, ADR-016 and B21 (both amended in S11.1's commit):
  (1) **Slide masters**: each .pptx gets a master and one layout per kind of slide (cover "Title Slide", contents,
  content "Title Only", and "Blank"), made from the deck itself: the title, subtitle, footer and page-number
  placeholders where its slides have them, the background its slides share (Liquid Glass's wallpaper: one picture for
  the whole file), the decoration every slide of a kind shares (drawn once, on its layout), and a theme with the design's
  colours (its chart palette as accents) and fonts. Slides hold only what is theirs. (2) **No bulk pictures**: the
  per-slide background PNG and the PNG per glass card of 07 §4.1 are dropped – everything is drawn natively from the
  display list: Liquid Glass's frosted surfaces are the one blurred wallpaper picture, cropped to each surface's rounded
  outline, under its tint, rim and sheens as gradient shapes; shadows are the shapes' own (PowerPoint's blur); capsules
  stay rounded shapes behind real tables. The only pictures are the workbook's pictures, the logo and the wallpaper,
  each stored once. (3) **No connections**: no connector shapes (lines are line shapes or table borders) and no link
  outside the file (charts embed their data; nothing is linked). (4) **Tables**: every table is a real table – cell
  fills, borders and separators on the cells, its texts in them; text that cannot fit a cell in PowerPoint (it runs past
  it or is turned) is a text box over the table, in the table's group. Measured on this PC's PowerPoint 16 while
  building it: every corpus deck × design opens in it and passes pptxcheck's lint (PowerPoint's open alone does not
  catch every repair – the S11 review showed it).

- 2026-10-12 · Decisions (product owner, given to the agent to record: "make the decisions" after the pre-M3 review,
  `docs/next/review-pre-M3.md`):
  1. **M2 is closed with a follow-up** (the "M2 close-out" line above): the review's bugs are fixed before M3; their
     user-visible effects (CHANGELOG 3.7.0, "What users notice") are approved changes besides B1–B14.
  2. **Bench gates:** the memory budget counts the page and its core worker together; the tasks from Finish until
     the first slide is drawn are gated at 250 ms (decision 1.a's window keeps its own, looser limit; 150 ms was tried first and failed once on a GitHub runner at 179 ms while the other run took 55 ms – the gate is there to catch a freeze, v3 had 112–154 ms).
  3. **Text measurement for the display list (S3.3, S3.5):** until S4.4 measures from font files, the text of the
     display list is measured by the page's browser layout: `TextMeasurer` gains `measureRuns(runs, cssFont) →
     advances` and `breakLines(text, cssFont, width, …) → lines`, implemented on the page (canvas `measureText` and
     the DOM, like `noteFits`) and called by the core worker through the RPC, batched per table like `digitWidth`;
     the worker's `noteFits` asks the page too. No page task over 50 ms (bench). OffscreenCanvas in the worker
     passes in Chromium 153 on the reference PC but is unverified in Edge (`field-results.md` §2), so it is not
     relied on yet. Prompt 4 (S3.1, S3.3, S3.4, its protocol) and 05 §1 (GF) are amended accordingly.
  4. **Display-list contract:** ADR-004 as amended on 2026-10-12 (blend multiply, inset shadows, elliptical radial
     paints, image-alpha drop shadows, title text shadows). Soft shadows use **5** layers (v3's `printcss.ts`
     STEPS = 5, today's PDFs), not 6: Prompt 4 S3.4 and S4.1's "6-layer" read "5-layer".
  5. **PDF text check (GF, Prompt 4 protocol step 3):** the extracted text is compared with the golden slide texts
     (GT, `slide-<n>.json` "text"), not with v3's PDF text, which is unreadable for glass and part of clean.
  6. **Acrobat recipe in S3.1:** soft masks (above): Prompt 4 S3.1's "the flattened-gradient recipe if G0 requires
     it" is not done.
  7. **Clean clones on Windows:** "/tmp/verify-<step>" means a folder under `%TEMP%`; npm there needs
     `NODE_OPTIONS=--use-system-ca` behind the proxy (README §11).

- **M5a deviations and limits, recorded for the product owner:** the helper's journal listing is cached 3 s (04 says
  10 s: stacked with SMB's 10 s it made newcomers' records wait up to 20 s); config.json is no longer "style.patch
  only" applied by the helper (04 §1): the page computes `defaults.style` and the helper stores it by compare-and-set
  on `rev` under the same lock (v3's edits are never lost, tested with the real v3 helper); the v2 import does not
  keep v2's `updated` time; B4: same-field edits that straddle a compaction are ordered causally (30 s margin) but two
  truly concurrent ones may resolve by arrival, and a page whose tails failed longer than the margin can bring back a
  value somebody replaced (ARCHITECTURE §5.1; nothing is lost, every page converges); a failed first clock probe on a
  PC whose clock is off stamps `at` with that PC's clock (rare: the share would refuse the probe file).
